Regular video meetings can help foster a sense of connection and rapport among team members.Finally, to mitigate the challenge of technical jargon, make an active effort to explain complex concepts in simpler terms, especially to non-technical stakeholders. Encourage questions and foster an environment where it is okay to ask for clarification.
Staying ahead of the technology curve
The pace of change in the realm of cloud-native technologies is rapid and relentless. Whether it's the introduction of new tools, the evolution of existing technologies, or shifts in best practices, staying ahead of the technology curve is an integral part of being a successful platform engineer.The cloud-native landscape
(figure 7.7)
is vast and varied, with a constant stream of innovations offering new possibilities for infrastructure, application deployment, automation, and scalability. From the advancement of container technologies and orchestration tools to the advent of serverless computing, these shifts have profound implications for how we design and manage platforms.
Figure 7.7 – The CNCF landscape (landscape.cncf.io)
As businesses evolve, so do their technology needs. New challenges may require different technologies or approaches. As platform engineers, we are often at the forefront of driving technology strategy, using our knowledge and skills to assess how new technologies can enable business objectives. Platform engineering, as we've discussed in previous chapters, plays a pivotal role in this process.Staying updated with the latest technology trends is essential for a number of reasons. First, it allows us to identify tools and techniques that can improve efficiency, security, and developer experience. Second, it provides insight into the future direction of the industry, enabling us to make informed decisions that will stand the test of time. Third, it equips us with the knowledge needed to engage effectively with other technical professionals, ensuring we can contribute to discussions and decisions at all levels of the organization.There are a number of ways platform engineers can stay on top of the latest trends:
Community involvement
: Attend meetups, webinars, and conferences. Join professional groups or communities related to your field.
Hands-on experimentation
: Try out new technologies in a sandbox environment or contribute to open-source projects. This provides hands-on experience and a deeper understanding of the technology.
Continuous learning
: Engage in formal training or certification programs. Many technology providers offer training on their products.
Evaluating new technologies and integrating them into your platform involves a multi-step process:
Assess relevance
: Determine if the technology aligns with business goals and needs.
Pilot and evaluate
: Test the technology in a controlled environment and assess its performance and applicability to your needs.
Plan integration
: If the technology proves beneficial, develop a plan for integration that considers potential impacts on existing systems and workflows.
Implement and monitor
: Implement the new technology, monitor its performance, and adjust as necessary.
Staying current can be challenging due to the sheer volume of information and the pace of change. It can also be difficult to find the time to learn amidst the demands of daily work. However, these challenges can be addressed. For example, setting aside dedicated
learning time
each week can be an effective strategy. Joining communities of practice can also be beneficial, as learning can be shared and distributed among group members.A culture of learning encourages continuous growth and adaptation, creating an environment where individuals are motivated to learn and apply their new knowledge. Here are some strategies to foster such a culture:
Learning time
: Allocate a certain amount of time each week for learning activities. This can include reading, attending webinars, experimenting with new tools, or completing online courses.
Knowledge sharing
: Encourage team members to share their new knowledge with the team. This could take the form of presentations, workshops, or informal chats.
Mentorship and pair programming
: Encourage experienced team members to mentor less experienced ones. Pair programming, where two engineers work together on the same task, is another great way to share knowledge.
Learning resources
: Provide access to learning resources such as online courses, books, and industry conferences
Expanding the platform team's knowledge and staying ahead of the curve is a very important starting point. As a next step, platform teams need to find ways to continuously integrate new tools into their platform.
Evaluating and integrating new technologies
As the cloud native ecosystem continues to expand and evolve, so does the pool of potential technologies that could enhance or streamline your platform. However, with the myriad of options available, it becomes crucial to have a methodical process to evaluate these new technologies, followed by a seamless integration plan.Evaluation of new technologies should be strategic and deliberate, with a view to addressing specific issues or enhancing certain aspects of the platform. Here are the suggested steps to follow:
Define requirements
: Start by outlining the needs or issues that the new technology is expected to address. This sets a basis for what features or capabilities you should look out for in potential solutions.
Research
: Identify potential technologies that could meet the requirements. Consider using resources like technology blogs, developer forums, or consulting with industry peers to generate a list of options.
Evaluate
: Investigate the chosen technologies in more depth, taking into account factors such as ease of use, compatibility with existing systems, cost, community support, and the long-term viability of the technology.
Test
: If possible, test the technology in a controlled environment. This could be a proof of concept or a pilot project. Testing provides practical insights that can further inform the decision-making process.
The integration of a new technology should be a planned and careful process:
Planning
: Develop a detailed integration plan that outlines the steps to be taken, including any changes to be made to the existing system, the resources needed, and a timeline for the integration.
Preparation
: Prepare the existing system for the integration. This might involve updating certain aspects of the platform, training the team on the new technology, or setting up the necessary infrastructure.
Implementation
: Integrate the new technology according to the plan. Monitor the process closely to ensure everything goes as expected and to identify and address any issues that arise promptly.
Testing and verification
: Once the technology has been integrated, conduct thorough testing to ensure it functions as expected and does not adversely affect the platform's performance or functionality.
The integration of a new technology into an existing platform is often accompanied by risks and challenges, such as potential compatibility issues, disruptions to the existing system, and resistance from the team.To mitigate these risks, it is essential to have a comprehensive integration plan, complete with a risk assessment and mitigation strategies. Regular communication with the team is also key to managing changes and overcoming resistance. Additionally, providing adequate training can ease the transition and increase the team's comfort level with the new technology.In the dynamic world of platform engineering, the ability to evaluate and integrate new technologies efficiently is a critical skill. A meticulous approach to this process not only ensures that the right technologies are adopted but also that their integration is smooth and effective.
Summary
In this chapter, we dove deep into the process and strategy of building an effective platform engineering team. We started with an exploration of Conway's Law and its implications on the structure and design of organizational teams. This principle guided our discussion as we dissected how the organizational structure often mirrors the design of the systems being built.We then moved onto the concept of fit-for-purpose teams, and how aligning the team's structure, skills, and objectives to the needs of the organization can yield significant benefits. In the context of platform engineering, we discovered how aligning the team's design to the four pillars - developer experience, increased productivity, ensuring security and compliance, and promoting best practices - can enhance the team's effectiveness.In our exploration of team topologies, we looked at how different configurations of team structures impact communication, workflow, and overall performance. Here, we highlighted the versatility of platform engineering teams, showing how they can function across different topologies.Then, we examined the role of the platform engineer in depth. We emphasized that a platform engineer is more akin to a software engineer than to a DevOps engineer, given that they're building a product of their own - the internal developer platform. The need for a diverse background, a clear career path, and a good balance of soft skills was also brought to the forefront.Finally, we looked at the necessity for platform engineers to stay ahead of the technology curve. As the landscape of cloud-native technologies continues to evolve at a rapid pace, we discussed strategies for continuous learning, integrating new technologies into the platform, and fostering a culture of continuous development.Through understanding and applying these principles and strategies, organizations can build platform engineering teams that are well-equipped to face the challenges of the modern, cloud-native world.In the next chapter we'll start exploring the concept of platform adoption and the importance of early adopters and quick wins in any large-scale change, such as the
transition
to platform engineering.

11 Leading Platform Engineering
Join our book community on Discord
https://packt.link/IOzft
In this chapter, we explore the role of a platform leader, highlighting the skills and strategies necessary to drive platform engineering success. Leading a platform engineering team requires balancing technical leadership with effective people management, adopting a product mindset, and fostering internal communities. Here, we provide practical guidance on how to achieve these objectives and avoid common pitfalls.By the end of this chapter, you will have a comprehensive understanding of the key aspects of platform leadership. You will learn how to build and manage a high-performing platform engineering team, provide technical direction, treat the platform as a product, and create a collaborative culture through community building. These lessons are crucial for ensuring that the platform delivers value and supports your organization's strategic goals.In this chapter, we're going to cover the following main topics:
The multiple roles of a platform leader
Technical leadership
Platform as product
Building internal communities for platform growth
The multiple roles of a platform leader
Leading a platform engineering team means wearing many hats, each with unique responsibilities and challenges. A platform leader must balance technical acumen with managerial skills, adopt a product-focused mindset, and foster community within the organization. This multifaceted role requires a deep understanding of both the technology and the people who use and build it.To better understand what makes a successful platform leader, let's explore these different roles in detail:
As
a people manager
, the platform leader plays a vital role in shaping team dynamics and performance. This includes recruiting and retaining top talent, fostering a collaborative and inclusive culture, and providing the necessary resources and support. Effective management also involves recognizing individual strengths and offering growth opportunities. Regular one-on-one meetings, team-building activities, and clear communication of goals are essential for team members to build trust and create an environment where everyone feels valued and empowered. Building trust through personal connections and celebrating achievements are key to keeping the team motivated. Additionally, the platform leader must act as a bridge between the team and the wider organization, advocating for the team's needs, managing cross-functional relationships, and ensuring alignment with broader organizational goals. Through strong people management, a platform leader can build a high-performing team capable of delivering exceptional results.
At the heart of platform leadership is
Technical leadership
through a technical vision and strategy. The platform leader is responsible for making key architectural decisions and ensuring best practices are followed. Staying up to date with technological trends and guiding the team toward the most effective tools is crucial. One key principle is avoiding over-engineering – focusing on a
"Thinnest Viable Platform" (TVP)
that meets essential requirements without unnecessary complexity. Derived from the
"Team Topologies"
framework, the TVP emphasizes delivering core capabilities while enabling innovation. Technical leadership also involves ensuring the platform is flexible and scalable, supporting integration with existing systems and future scalability. Promoting a modular architecture and fostering continuous improvement, is essential for long-term success
Adopting
a product mindset
is crucial for the platform leader. Viewing the platform as a product, with its own lifecycle, roadmap, and customer base, is essential for its success. The platform leader must understand user needs, which often include internal development teams, and prioritize features that deliver value. This involves conducting a thorough assessment, defining a clear vision and goals, and iterating based on feedback. A deep understanding of product management principles is required to balance short-term achievements with long-term objectives. Monitoring
key performance indicators (KPIs)
, such as user satisfaction, adoption rates, and the platform's impact on productivity, helps guide decision-making. Treating the platform as a product ensures that it remains relevant and valuable.
Building
a strong community
around the platform is essential. By creating a vibrant internal community around the platform encourages collaboration, innovation, and continuous improvement. A platform leader should establish an environment where users and contributors are empowered to share ideas and provide feedback. This can be achieved through inner sourcing, where internal teams collaborate in an open-source-like model. Inner sourcing promotes shared ownership, transparency, and collective problem-solving, leading to higher-quality solutions. Facilitating communication through regular meetings, forums, and documentation helps keep stakeholders aligned. Additionally, fostering a learning culture through workshops, training sessions, and hackathons allows team members to develop their skills and stay current with technological advancements. Building a strong community around the platform helps harness the collective expertise of the organization, driving its ongoing success.
Embracing these various responsibilities enables you to effectively guide your team, foster innovation, and ensure the continuous evolution of the platform
Technical leadership
Technical leadership in platform engineering is about setting the vision, direction, and standards for the engineering team while ensuring that the platform evolves in alignment with the organization's needs. It involves making strategic decisions about the architecture, tools, and processes used to build and maintain the platform. A strong technical leader must balance innovation with practicality, ensuring that the platform remains scalable, reliable, and easy to use without succumbing to unnecessary complexity.Effective technical leadership starts with a deep understanding of the current technology landscape and the specific challenges faced by the organization. This knowledge allows the leader to make informed decisions about which technologies and methodologies to adopt. It also involves fostering a culture of continuous learning and improvement, where team members are encouraged to stay up-to-date with the latest developments and share their knowledge with others.A key aspect of technical leadership is maintaining a clear and consistent vision for the platform. This vision should be communicated regularly to the team and other stakeholders to ensure alignment and buy-in. It is also essential to establish and enforce best practices for coding, testing, deployment, and maintenance, ensuring that the platform remains robust and efficient as it grows.
Don't over engineer your platform
As previously mentioned, one of the biggest pitfalls in platform engineering is over-engineering. This occurs when the platform becomes overly complex and difficult to manage, often due to an excessive focus on edge cases and hypothetical future requirements. Over-engineering not only increases the development and maintenance burden but also makes the platform harder to use and less adaptable to change.To avoid over-engineering, it's crucial to adopt the concept of the Thinnest Viable Platform (TVP). According to the Team Topologies framework, a TVP is a minimal platform that provides just enough functionality to support the team's needs without adding unnecessary complexity. This approach emphasizes starting small and iterating based on user feedback and actual usage patterns. The idea of a TVP aligns with the principles of
Agile development
, where the focus is on delivering value incrementally and responding to change rather than trying to predict and build for every possible scenario upfront. By starting with a minimal viable platform, teams can quickly deliver value and make adjustments based on real-world feedback, ensuring that the platform remains lean and efficient.The concept of the TVP is central to avoiding over-engineering. The TVP is about providing the minimum necessary capabilities to enable product teams to deliver value independently. It should offer essential services and tools that streamline development without imposing unnecessary constraints or complexity. A TVP should focus on core functionalities that address immediate needs while allowing for incremental improvements. This approach helps maintain flexibility and responsiveness, enabling the platform to evolve in line with user requirements and technological advancements.Starting small allows you to deliver quick wins and gather feedback, which informs subsequent iterations of the platform. By focusing on essential capabilities and avoiding premature optimization, a team can maintain a lean and adaptable platform. Building a TVP ensures that development teams are not burdened with unnecessary complexity, allowing them to focus on delivering value. It also reduces the risk of platform lock-in and makes it easier to pivot as requirements change.While the
Minimum Viable Product
(
MVP
) focuses on delivering the smallest set of features that provide value, the TVP is about providing the minimal set of platform capabilities that enable teams to work efficiently. Combining both concepts ensures that the platform remains focused on delivering value without becoming overburdened with unnecessary features.
Designing the platform
Designing a successful platform involves balancing flexibility with opinionated defaults. It's essential to provide developers with the tools and capabilities they need to work efficiently while avoiding unnecessary restrictions that could stifle innovation.One of the biggest mistakes in platform design is imposing too many restrictions on developers. While it's important to enforce certain standards and golden paths, overly rigid constraints can limit creativity and reduce productivity. Developers should have the freedom to choose the tools and approaches that work best for their specific use cases.By providing
opinionated defaults
that can be overwritten, platforms can offer guidance without being prescriptive. This approach allows developers to benefit from best practices while retaining the flexibility to adapt to their unique needs.Opinionated defaults provide a starting point for developers, offering sensible configurations and workflows that align with best practices. However, it's crucial to allow these defaults to be customized or overridden. This balance ensures that developers can work efficiently while retaining the flexibility to tailor the platform to their specific requirements. Providing opinionated defaults helps streamline the development process, reduce setup time, and ensure consistency across projects. At the same time, allowing these defaults to be customized ensures that the platform remains adaptable and responsive to changing needs.When developers are forced to use specific tools or workflows, platform lock-in occurs, making it difficult to adapt to new technologies or approaches. This lock-in can stifle innovation and reduce the platform's long-term value. To avoid lock-in, it's essential to design the platform in a way that allows for flexibility and choice. By providing multiple options and ensuring interoperability with a wide range of tools and services, platforms can reduce the risk of lock-in and increase their value. This approach allows teams to take advantage of the best available technologies and adapt to changing requirements without being constrained by the platform.Offering a range of options increases the platform's value by allowing teams to choose the tools and workflows that best meet their needs. This flexibility ensures that the platform can support a wide range of use cases and adapt to evolving requirements. It also encourages innovation by allowing teams to experiment with new approaches and technologies. A successful platform should provide a robust set of core capabilities while remaining open and extensible. This approach ensures that the platform can grow and evolve in line with the organization's needs, delivering long-term value and supporting continuous improvement.Technical leadership in platform engineering involves balancing the need for robust and scalable infrastructure with the flexibility and innovation required to meet evolving user needs. By focusing on the TVP, avoiding over-engineering, and providing flexible and opinionated defaults, platform leaders can create a lean, efficient, and adaptable platform that drives value for the organization. Next up is focusing on internal customers' needs while treating the platform as a customer facing product.
Platform as product
Treating the platform as a product has emerged as a critical strategy for delivering sustained value to organizations. This approach is rooted in adopting a product mindset, which emphasizes understanding and meeting the needs of the platform's users—typically internal developers and teams—through continuous improvement and strategic planning.Treating the platform as a product ensures that it evolves to genuinely solve real problems and deliver tangible value to users. Product mindset moves beyond merely building and maintaining infrastructure to actively managing and enhancing a product that must meet user demands and expectations.A product mindset in platform engineering goes beyond technical specifications, focusing on understanding the user journey, identifying pain points, and iterating on the platform to improve the user experience. This approach encourages platform teams to:This product-driven mindset ensures the platform remains aligned with the organization's objectives and adapts to users' evolving needs. It fosters a culture of continuous improvement, with the platform team always looking for ways to enhance the user experience and drive greater value.Product thinking extends the product mindset into actionable strategies and practices. It views the platform as a product with its own lifecycle, roadmap, and customer base. In the following section, we will cover the key principles underlying a product mindset and how to adopt this mindset
Adopting a product mindset
Adopting a product mindset transforms platform engineering from merely managing infrastructure to delivering a product that provides tangible value. This shift involves understanding user needs, prioritizing high-impact features, and continuously iterating based on feedback. It also requires aligning the platform's development with the principles of product management to ensure it evolves in a way that benefits users and the broader organization.As outlined earlier, product thinking views the platform as a product with its own lifecycle, user base, and strategic goals. This approach emphasizes:
User-centric development
: The platform must be designed with its users in mind, understanding their workflows, challenges, and requirements. Regularly interact with users to understand their needs and gather feedback and insights through surveys, interviews, user testing, and performance data analysis to evaluate platform success. This helps identify pain points and opportunities for improvement.
Iterative improvement
: Instead of aiming for perfection upfront, the platform team should release features incrementally and adjust based on real-world usage, ensuring relevance and effectiveness over time. Employ agile methodologies to iteratively develop and release platform updates, ensuring that each version adds value and meets user needs.
Prioritization of features
: Use user feedback and strategic goals to prioritize features that deliver the most value and address critical issues. This involves balancing short-term needs with long-term strategic goals.
Measurement of success
: Establish KPIs and metrics that reflect user satisfaction, adoption rates, and the platform's impact on productivity and business outcomes. Use these metrics to inform decisions and demonstrate value.
Value delivery
: Every feature should deliver tangible value to users by addressing their most pressing needs and providing high impact. This requires a clear understanding of the platform's value proposition and the ability to measure the impact of new features.
Strategic road mapping
: Outline short-term, medium-term, and long-term goals, with specific features and enhancements to be delivered over time. A flexible yet well-defined roadmap aligns the platform's evolution with organizational goals while remaining adaptable to changing user needs and technological advancements. It helps in aligning the platform's development with the organization's strategic objectives. Continuously refine the platform by updating the roadmap and adapting to evolving user needs and organizational goals.
Vision and goal communication
: Clearly communicate the platform's vision, goals, and roadmap to stakeholders to ensure alignment and understanding across teams. Regular updates on progress and roadmap changes are also essential.
Benefits of a product mindset
Enhanced user satisfaction
: Focusing on user needs and iterating based on feedback ensures the platform meets expectations and improves productivity.
Increased adoption and engagement
: A user-centric platform that addresses real pain points is more likely to be adopted and actively used by internal teams, leading to greater engagement.
Alignment with business goals
: A strategic roadmap aligned with organizational objectives ensures the platform contributes to broader business outcomes, securing support and resources for further development.
Continuous improvement
: An iterative approach allows for ongoing enhancements and adaptation to changing needs and technologies, keeping the platform relevant and effective.
Challenges in adopting a product mindset
While the benefits of adopting a product mindset are significant, challenges can arise. One major challenge is ensuring the platform team has the necessary skills to embrace product thinking, which may require training in product management principles and practices.Additionally, balancing the needs of different user groups and stakeholders is often difficult. The platform team must prioritize features that deliver the most value while managing conflicting requirements, making effective communication and stakeholder management critical.By adopting a product mindset and product thinking principles, platform teams can drive higher user satisfaction, increase adoption, and ensure the platform aligns with organizational goals, delivering long-term value.
Developing a platform strategy
Developing a robust platform strategy is critical for ensuring that the platform meets user needs, aligns with organizational objectives, and evolves effectively over time.The first step in this process is mapping the current state of the platform and the organization's infrastructure. Key elements of this current state mapping include:
Infrastructure and tools
: Document the existing infrastructure, covering hardware, software, and cloud services. Assess the tools and technologies currently in use to gauge their effectiveness and efficiency.
Processes and workflows
: Evaluate development, deployment, and operational processes. Identify bottlenecks or inefficiencies that may be causing pain points.
User needs and feedback
: Gather feedback from platform users –typically internal developers and teams– through surveys, interviews, and testing. Understand their requirements and pain points.
Performance metrics
: Analyze existing metrics like uptime, performance, user satisfaction, and business outcomes to gauge how well the platform is meeting its goals.
By thoroughly mapping the current state, platform teams can identify improvement opportunities and establish a baseline for tracking future progress.With this foundation, the next step is to define the platform approach. This involves creating a clear vision and goals that align with the organization's long-term objectives. The platform approach serves as a strategic guide for development efforts and outlines key principles and strategies.Key aspects of the platform approach include:
Vision and goals
: Define a clear vision for what the platform aims to achieve, and set
S
pecific,
M
easurable,
A
chievable,
R
elevant, and
T
ime-bound (
SMART
) goals. The platform SMART goals should look forward and aim to facilitate the product goals that will follow a few quarter later.
Principles and best practices
: Establish guiding principles, including adopting industry standards, promoting modularity, ensuring security, and maintaining compliance.
User-centric design
: Focus on designing the platform with the users in mind, continually engaging with them to gather feedback and ensure the platform meets their evolving needs.
Technology stack
: Select the technologies, tools, and frameworks that will support platform scalability, functionality, and scalability.
Governance and compliance
: Define roles, responsibilities, policies, and procedures to ensure governance and compliance with relevant standards and regulations.
Defining the platform approach ensures that the development aligns with organizational goals and strategic priorities.Developing a platform is not a one-time effort; it requires an iterative approach. The platform roadmap outlines planned activities, milestones, and timelines, and should be treated as a living document that adapts to feedback, performance metrics, and changing requirements.Measuring platform success is a crucial part of ensuring its continued value and impact. This requires defining key performance indicators (KPIs) and metrics to evaluate how well the platform is achieving its goals. Measuring success also provides critical insights for guiding future development. For more details, see Chapter 12 where we explore measuring platform success further.Key aspects of measuring platform success include:
Defining KPIs and metrics
: Establish metrics related to platform performance, user satisfaction, adoption rates, and business outcomes.
Data collection and analysis
: Implement systems for monitoring and collecting relevant data, using analytics tools to understand performance and usage patterns.
Reporting and communication
: Communicate platform performance to stakeholders through regular reports, using visual tools like dashboards to make the data accessible and actionable.
Feedback and iteration
: Leverage insights from performance data and user feedback to refine and improve the platform continuously.
By effectively measuring platform success, teams can make data-driven decisions, demonstrate value to stakeholders, and ensure that the platform continues to meet user needs while supporting organizational goals.
Leading the transformation
Leading the transformation to a platform-as-product approach requires a comprehensive strategy that addresses various facets of the organization. This includes driving platform adoption, fostering a supportive human capital and culture, establishing an effective operating model, managing change, and ensuring robust governance and compliance. Each of these elements is critical for ensuring a successful transformation and maximizing the platform's value to the organization.Driving platform adoption is one of the primary challenges and responsibilities of platform leadership. Ensuring that internal teams not only use the platform but also derive significant value from it is essential. The following strategies can be pivotal:
Clear Communication
: Articulating the benefits and functionalities of the platform to all stakeholders is crucial. This involves regular demonstrations, training sessions, and comprehensive documentation that highlights how the platform can improve productivity and streamline workflows.
Support and Onboarding
: Providing robust support during the onboarding phase is essential. This includes offering tutorials, hands-on workshops, and dedicated support channels to help teams get started and troubleshoot issues as they arise.
Champion Programs
: Identifying and nurturing platform champions within different teams can facilitate broader adoption. These champions can act as ambassadors, helping to promote the platform's benefits and assist their peers in utilizing it effectively.
Incentives and Recognition
: Implementing a system of incentives and recognition for teams and individuals who actively adopt and contribute to the platform can drive engagement and foster a culture of collaboration and continuous improvement.
The success of a platform-as-product approach heavily relies on the people who build, maintain, and use the platform. Therefore, focusing on human capital and fostering a supportive culture is crucial.
Skill Development
: Investing in the continuous learning and development of the platform team is vital. This includes providing training on new technologies, encouraging participation in industry conferences, and supporting certification programs.
Collaboration and Teamwork
: Promoting a culture of collaboration and teamwork within the platform team and across the organization ensures that knowledge and expertise are shared. Regular cross-functional meetings, hackathons, and collaborative projects can facilitate this.
Diversity and Inclusion
: Building a diverse and inclusive team brings different perspectives and ideas, which can drive innovation and improve problem-solving. Ensuring that hiring practices and workplace policies promote diversity and inclusion is essential.
Employee Engagement
: Engaging employees by involving them in decision-making processes, acknowledging their contributions, and providing opportunities for career growth can lead to higher job satisfaction and retention rates.
Establishing an effective operating model is critical for managing the platform's lifecycle and ensuring its success. This involves defining clear roles, responsibilities, processes, and tools to support platform development, deployment, and maintenance:
Roles and responsibilities
: Clearly defining roles within the platform team and across the organization ensures accountability and streamlines workflows. This includes platform owners, developers, operators, and support staff.
Processes and workflows
: Standardized across the whole SDLC. Agile methodologies and CI/CD practices enhance efficiency and adaptability.
Tools and technologies
: Selecting the right tools and technologies to support the platform's goals is crucial. This includes tools for version control, project management, monitoring, and security.
Service-Level Agreements (SLAs)
: Establishing SLAs helps set performance expectations and provides a framework for measuring service quality.
Managing change effectively is crucial for a smooth transition to the platform-as-product approach. Change management involves preparing, supporting, and guiding individuals and teams to adopt and adapt to new platforms and processes.
Change readiness assessment
: Assessing organizational readiness helps identify potential resistance and areas needing additional support, considering factors such as company culture, previous change efforts, and stakeholder attitudes.
Stakeholder engagement
: Engaging stakeholders early and throughout the process ensures their support. Regular communication, involvement in decision-making, and addressing concerns are key to maintaining engagement.
Training and support
: Providing comprehensive training and ongoing support through workshops, e-learning, and dedicated channels ensures teams can adapt to new platforms and processes.
Monitoring and feedback
: Continuous monitoring and feedback from users and stakeholders enable course correction and adjustments, keeping the transformation on track and achieving the desired outcomes.
Ensuring robust governance and compliance is essential for maintaining platform integrity, security, and reliability. This involves establishing policies and controls that align with industry standards and regulatory requirements:
Governance framework
: A clear governance framework defines roles, responsibilities, and decision-making processes, covering areas such as risk management, compliance, and performance monitoring.
Security and privacy
: Implementing security best practices, conducting regular audits, and complying with regulations like the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) and the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA) is essential to maintaining platform security and privacy.
Compliance management
: Establishing processes for managing compliance with industry standards helps avoid legal or financial penalties. Regular reviews and prompt issue resolution are critical.
Continuous improvement
: Governance and compliance should be dynamic, evolving alongside the platform and the organization. Regular reviews and updates of policies ensure continued relevance and effectiveness.
Leading a platform-as-product transformation requires addressing platform adoption, human capital, operating models, change management, and governance and compliance. By focusing on these areas, platform leaders can ensure sustained value, meet user needs, and guide the platform's evolution over time.
Building internal communities for platform growth
Building internal communities is essential for the success and growth of platform engineering. These communities foster collaboration, knowledge sharing, and innovation, which are critical for the continuous improvement and adoption of the platform. Implementing innersourcing is a powerful strategy to achieve these goals. Innersourcing applies open source principles within an organization, enabling teams to collaborate on shared projects and contribute to each other's work. This approach can significantly enhance the development and evolution of the platform by leveraging the collective expertise and creativity of the entire organization.
Implementing innersourcing for platform engineering
Innersourcing
involves adopting open-source methodologies within a corporate environment. It encourages transparency, collaboration, and shared ownership of projects across different teams. By treating internal projects as open source, organizations can break down silos, promote cross-functional collaboration, and accelerate innovation. Innersourcing fosters a culture of openness and inclusivity, where contributions from all team members are valued and encouraged.Implementing innersourcing in platform engineering offers several benefits. It enhances the quality and robustness of the platform by leveraging diverse perspectives and expertise. Collaborative development helps identify and resolve issues more efficiently, leading to higher-quality code and faster delivery of features. Innersourcing also fosters a sense of community and shared ownership, increasing engagement and motivation among team members.Under the Inner Source operating model, platform engineers act as maintainers or trusted committers of the core repositories—responsible for architecture, consistency, and code quality—while other teams participate as contributors. A very important role in InnerSource is that of the Trusted Commiter. A Trusted Committer helps push contributions through the finish line, maintain quality, and engage the community. These relatinships and responsibilities are shown in the image below.
In practice, the platform team publishes well-documented, structured repositories for internal tools, such as infrastructure modules, CLI utilities, or CI/CD templates. These repositories include contribution guidelines, coding standards, a well-defined branching strategy, and test automation to ensure safe contributions. Developers from feature or product teams identify improvements or new features they need and open Pull Requests (PRs) against the shared codebase.PRs undergo the same rigorous review process as in open source: platform engineers review and approve the changes, ensuring alignment with architectural patterns and reliability standards. Over time, trusted contributors from other teams may be given more autonomy—such as merging rights or roadmap influence—as part of a federated contribution model.The product roadmap remains owned by the platform team but evolves with input from the wider organization. An open issues board and regular triage meetings help prioritize contributions. Feature proposals can be submitted via design docs or GitHub Discussions, where maintainers evaluate feasibility and impact.Ultimately, inner sourcing enables the platform team to scale its impact by turning every engineering team into a contributor to the internal developer platform ecosystem, while also keeping everyone engaged which also impacts platform adoption.
Summary
In this chapter, we explored the multifaceted role of a platform leader, focusing on the critical areas of people management, technical leadership, treating the platform as a product, and fostering internal communities. We discussed the importance of balancing these roles to drive platform engineering success, emphasizing the need for strong leadership skills and strategic vision.You learned about the value of building and managing a high-performing team, providing clear technical direction without over-engineering, adopting a product mindset to align platform development with user needs, and creating a collaborative culture through community building. These skills are essential for ensuring that the platform delivers continuous value and supports your organization's strategic goals.In the next chapter, we will delve into measuring platform success. We will explore the metrics and methodologies for evaluating the performance and impact of your platform, building on the leadership strategies discussed in this chapter to ensure your platform not only meets but exceeds its objectives.

As organizations strive to optimize their development processes and infrastructure, having robust metrics and KPIs becomes essential. This chapter aims to equip you with the knowledge and tools needed to measure platform engineering success comprehensively.Throughout this chapter, you will learn about the distinctions between outputs, outcomes, and impacts, and how these concepts interrelate to provide a holistic view of productivity and performance. We will explore various frameworks and metrics, including DORA, SPACE, and DevEx, that offer valuable insights into different facets of platform engineering. Additionally, you will discover advanced KPIs that go beyond traditional metrics, providing a more nuanced understanding of platform engineering success.By the end of this chapter, you will be able to implement effective measurement strategies, utilize advanced KPIs, and create a comprehensive dashboard to track and report on platform performance. These lessons are designed to help you enhance the efficiency, reliability, and overall impact of your platform engineering initiatives.In this chapter, we're going to cover the following main topics:
Understanding Outputs, Outcomes, Impact
Measuring Cloud Native Maturity
